Detailed LP iteration algorithm:
Given current positive vector f length n, normalized sum = sqrt(2n) or 1. Let M = max convolution(f,f). Compute g0 = argmax sum g s.t. g≥0, f*g ≤ M (where convolution maybe full). This is a linear program. Rescale g to have same sum as f. Update f = (1-t) f + t g (or multiplicative?) and repeat. They claim nonincreasing M. If true, from any start can converge. We can implement with LP solver and choose t.

Function solve_lp(f, M):
  n=len(f)
  A = sparse matrix L=2n-1 x n. Fill columns: for j in range(n): rows k=j..j+n-1 values f[0..n-1]? Because (f*q)_k =∑_{j=0}^{n-1} f_{k-j} q_j if k-j valid. If p=f normalized sum1. So A_{k,j}=p[k-j] for 0≤k-j≤n-1. This is a lower-left? For j=0, rows 0..n-1 with p[0..n-1]. For j=1, rows1..n with p[0..n-1], etc. Thus each column is p shifted down.
  Build A using scipy.sparse.lil or csr:
    data = []
    For each column j, rows = np.arange(j, j+n), vals = p
    Stack. Or use diags: A has diagonals. Diagonal offset d = j? For row k, col j: d=k-j ranges 0..n-1 => A_{k-j,j}=p[d]. So columns are diagonals. Use diags([p[d] * (n-d)??]) with offsets? In scipy.spp diags, offset = k - j = d. Diagonal of offset d length n-d (column 0..n-d-1). Values p[d]. So A = sparse.diags([p[d] for d in range(n)], offsets=list(range(n)), shape=(2n-1,n), format='csc'). This works: offset 0 main length n; offset 1 length n-1 at rows j+1? For d=1, rows j+1 for j=0..n-2. Good. row count 2n-1. Then A*q length L.
  Objective max sum q => linprog(c=-ones, A_ub=A, b_ub=M*ones, bounds=(0,None), method='highs'). It may return infeasible? q=0 feasible. If sum q<=0? handle.

Implementation details for objective fast:
Define fast_score(seq):
  arr=np.asarray(seq,float)
  arr=np.clip(arr,0,None)
  S=arr.sum()
  if S<=0 return inf
  # normalize? objective scale invariant. Use arr/S.
  p=arr/S
  # convolution full via np.convolve(p,p) or FFT.
  For n up to 2048, np.convolve direct might be okay but we need many internal evaluations. Use FFT:
    m=1
    while m < 2*n-1: m*=2
    F=np.fft.rfft(p, m)
    C=np.fft.irfft(F*F, m)[:2*n-1]
    M=C.max()
    score=2*n*M  (since sum p=1). FFT errors tiny; for final exact use evaluate_sequence.
  Return score.
We must not import evaluate_sequence, but can call evaluate_sequence for final candidates. Internal use FFT.

```
import numpy as np, time, math
try:
 import scipy.optimize as opt
 import scipy.sparse as sp
 HAVE_SCIPY=True
except: HAVE_SCIPY=False

def fast_score(a):
  a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64); a=np.maximum(a,0)
  S=a.sum()
  if S<=0: return np.inf
  p=a/S
  n=p.size
  m=1
  while m < 2*n-1: m <<=1
  F=np.fft.rfft(p,m)
  c=np.fft.irfft(F*F,m)[:2*n-1]
  # due roundoff clip negatives
  # use np.max(c)
  return float(2*n*max(0.0,np.max(c)))

def exact_score(seq): return float(evaluate_sequence(seq))

def normalize(a, floor=0.0): a=np.maximum(a,0); s=a.sum()+floor*a.size? ...

def make_initial(n, rng): list of arrays
  uniform = ones
  geo_dec = exp(-a*x) with a in [0.5,1,1.5,2,3]
  geometric reverse
  maybe p = exp(-a*x) * (1+0.2 sin(...)?)
  polynomial: (x)^r for r? truncated maybe high endpoints? Many.
  from global height_sequence_1: resample to n by interpolation / averaging.
  random low-frequency: exp(∑ c_k cos(kx+φ)) with small coefficients, ensure positive.
  two-edge U-shape: exp(a*(x-0.5)^2 - b?)? symmetric maybe score≥2 but LP may make asymmetric.

def solve_lp(p, M, time_limit=...): assuming numpy/scipy.
  n=len(p); L=2n-1
  diags=[p[d] for d in range(n)]? Need ensure each diagonal length n-d. If pass arrays of length n? scipy.sparse.diags handles.
  A=sp.diags([p[d] for d in range(n)], offsets=list(range(n)), shape=(L,n), format='csc')
  cobj=-np.ones(n)
  b=M*np.ones(L)
  res=opt.linprog(cobj, A_ub=A, b_ub=b, bounds=[(0,None)]*n, method='highs')
  if res.success and res.x is not None: return clip res.x
  else maybe use greedy fallback.
```

Line search in LP iteration:
```
def lp_iteration(p, M, step_candidates=(0.05,0.1,0.2,0.4,0.7,1.0), smooth=True):
 q=solve_lp(p,M)
 if q is None or q.sum()<=0: return None
 q = q/q.sum()
 # optional smooth q
 if smooth:
   kernel = np.array([1,2,3,2,1],float); kernel/=sum
   q = np.convolve(q,kernel,'same'); q=np.maximum(q,0); q/=q.sum()
 best_r = 1.0? best_p=p; best_s=score(p)
 for r in steps:
   cand=(1-r)*p + r*q; cand[~finite]=0; cand=np.maximum(cand,0); s=cand.sum(); if s<=0 continue; cand/=s
   s=fast_score(cand)
   if s<best_s: best_p=cand; best_s=s
 return best_p, best_s
```

```
def subgradient_step(p, steps=100):
 C=conv_fft(p)
 M=C.max()
 K=np.where(C > M - 0.05*M)[0]
 # weights w=C/M
 grad = zeros
 for k in K:
   w=(C[k]-M*0.5)/(M*len?)
   valid i range max(0,k-n+1)..min(n-1,k)
   j=k-i
   grad[i] += w*2*p[j]
 # projected multiplicative:
 logp=log(p+eps)
 logp -= lr*(grad - grad.mean())
 p=exp(logp - max); p+=eps; normalize
```

def make_initials(n, rng):
 c=[]
 x=np.linspace(0,1,n)
 # uniform
 c.append(np.ones(n))
 # geometric parameter list
 for a in [0.3,0.5,0.7,1.0,1.3,1.8,2.5,3.5,5]:
   g=np.exp(-a*x); if sum>0 c.append(g); c.append(g[::-1])
 # two-sided asymmetric: exp(-a*x)+dec*exp(-b*(1-x))
 for lam,a,b in [(0.1,1,1),(0.2,1.5,1),(0.3,2,0.7),(0.4,3,1),(0.6,1,1)]:
   v=np.exp(-a*x)+lam*np.exp(-b*(1-x)); c.append(v)
 # Gaussian peak interior/edge:
 for mu,sig,a in [(0,1.0,1.0),(0.25,1.0,1.0),(0.5,1.0,1.0),(0.1,0.5,1.0),(0.9,0.5,1.0)]:
   v=np.exp(-a*((x-mu)/sig)**2); c.append(v)
 # polynomial/x powers
 for p in [0.5,1,2,3,4]: v=x**p; c.append(v); c.append((1-x)**p)
 # cosine phase with small amplitude to ensure positive:
 for amp,phi in [(0.1,0),(0.2,0.3),(0.4,0.5),(0.7,0.5),(0.9,0.5),(0.95,1.0)]:
   v=1+amp*np.cos(2*np.pi*x+phi); v=np.maximum(v,1e-6); c.append(v)
 # from global sequence resample
 arr = safe_global()
 if arr size>1: c.append(resample(arr,n))
 # random low-frequency:
 for _ in range(4):
   v=np.ones(n)
   for k in range(1,7):
      coef = rng.normal(0, 0.15*k? ); v += coef*(0.5*np.cos(2πkx+φ)+0.5*np.sin...)
   v=np.maximum(v,1e-3); c.append(v)
 return normalized unique
```

Let's implement Adam polish:
```
def polish_adam(p, time_limit):
 n=len(p); y=np.log(p+1e-30)
 m=np.zeros(n); v=np.zeros(n); t=0; lr=0.01
 alpha=1.0? We need target score in log? 
 while time<deadline:
   # compute C, M
   C=conv_fft_unnormalized(p) (p sum1)
   M=C.max()
   if M<=0 break
   logits=(C - M)/(max(M*0.1,1e-12))
   logits -= logits.max()
   w=np.exp(logits); w/=w.sum() # active weights, top near M get e^0=1, others small.
   G=np.zeros(n)
   # loop k where w>1e-3 maybe
   idx=np.nonzero(w>1e-4)[0]
   for k in idx:
      wk=2*w[k]
      # i valid max(0,k-n+1)..min(n-1,k)
      i0=max(0,k-n+1); i1=min(n-1,k)
      j0=k-i1; j1=k-i0
      G[i0:i1+1] += wk * p[j0:j1+1]
   # scaling G maybe /M? G dimension: w sum1, p sum1, G ~1/n? Gradient of LSE F=2n LSE; if logits scaled by 1/M, derivative includes 1/M. We used w normalized over scaled? Actually softmax of C/M: w_k. dLSE/dp_i=2 ∑ C_k/M? Wait if F=log(∑ exp(C_k/M)), derivative= (1/M)∑ exp(C_k/M)/Z * ∂C/∂p_i = (1/M)∑ w_k ∂C. We used w normalized; need multiply 1/M. Also factor 2. Score=2n F? For gradient direction, constant 2n/M. Use G = (2/M)∑ w_k p... ; then y_grad=p*(G - sumpG). 
   G *= 2.0 / M
   grad = p * (G - np.dot(p,G))
   # Adam update
   t+=1; m=β1*m+(1-β1)*grad; v=β2*v+(1-β2)*grad*grad
   mhat=m/(1-β1**t); vhat=v/(1-β2**t)
   y -= lr * mhat/(sqrt(vhat)+eps)
   pnew=exp(y - max); pnew += 1e-10; pnew/=sum
   if fast_score(pnew) < current - tiny: accept? Grad descent should, but nonconvex; line search maybe try lr and 0.5lr.
```

`conv_fft(a)`:
```
def conv_fft(a):
 n=a.size
 m=1
 target=2*n-1
 while m<target: m*=2
 F=np.fft.rfft(a,m)
 c=np.fft.irfft(F*F,m)[:target]
 c[np.abs(c)<1e-15]=0
 return c
```
For internal score, if a sum not1, first normalize.

```

`solve_lp(p, M, time_limit=None)`:
```
def solve_lp(p, M, max_n=None):
 try:
  if not HAVE_SCIPY: return None
  n=len(p); L=2*n-1
  diags=[float(p[d]) for d in range(n)]
  offsets=list(range(n))
  A=sp.diags(diags, offsets=offsets, shape=(L,n), format='csc')
  cobj=-np.ones(n)
  b=M*np.ones(L)
  bounds=[(0.0,None)]*n
  res=opt.linprog(cobj, A_ub=A, b_ub=b, bounds=bounds, method='highs')
  if res.success:
    q=np.asarray(res.x,float); q=np.maximum(q,0); q[np.isnan(q)|np.isinf(q)]=0
    if q.sum()>0: return q
  return None
 except Exception: return None
```
If M very small, b small; A q constraints. If M=0? p zero? not.
Potential issue: For n=2048, offsets list length 2048, diags scalar; `sp.diags` with scalars may create object? We can test in mind. scipy.sparse.diags accepts sequence of diagonals; each can be array_like. Scalars okay? It will treat as scalar diagonal? Documentation: If diagonals[m] is scalar, it will be repeated. Good.
But offset positive: length of main diag is n, offset1 length n-1; scalar repeated. Good.
If M is large, A_ub all M; if p has entries >? normalized p ≤1, M ~1/n. b ~1e-3. A entries maybe ~1/n or smaller. q values maybe ~1. Good.

Maybe the LP objective can be solved more efficiently by passing the transposed matrix? HiGHS may prefer constraints as A_ub. For max sum, it's fine. Could use `method='highs'` if not default. In SciPy 1.?, default 'highs'. We'll specify.

def lp_update(p, M, rng, smooth_sigma=0):
 q=solve_lp(p,M)
 if q is None: return None, None
 s=float(q.sum()); if s<=0 return None
 qq=q/s
 # optional blend with smoothed

   qb=smooth1d(qq, smooth_sigma)
   qq2=0.3*qb+0.7*qq
 else: qq2=qq
 qq2=np.maximum(qq2,0); s2=qq2.sum(); if s2<=0 return None; qq2/=s2
 # line search
 best_p=p.copy(); best_s=fast_score(p)
 rates=[0.05,0.1,0.2,0.35,0.5,0.75,1.0]

   ss=cand.sum(); if ss>0:
      cand/=ss; sc=fast_score(cand); if sc<best_s: ...
 # multiplicative for r in (0.2,0.4,0.7)

```
def perturb(p, rng):
  c=p.copy()
  # random smoothen/multiply block
  n=p.size
  width=int(rng.integers(2, max(3,n//16)))
  start=int(rng.integers(0,n-width))
  c[start:start+width] *= rng.normal(1.0, 0.2)
  if rng.random()<0.5 and n>2:
     c=0.5*(c+c[::-1])? symmetry hurts; maybe shift?
  return normalize(c,1e-10)
```

Let's write `adam_polish` carefully.
```
def adam_polish(p, deadline, state):
    n=len(p)
    p=normalize(p,1e-10)
    y=np.log(p+1e-30)
    m=np.zeros(n); v=np.zeros(n); t=0
    lr=1e-4; alpha=0.05
    best_s=fast_score(p); update state
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        C=conv_fft(p)
        M=float(np.max(C)) if C.size else 0
        if not finite or M<=0: break
        # weights: want top coefficients; logits = C/M - 1 maybe negative small
        logits=C/M - 1.0
        # To avoid overflow, max is 0, exp okay (1 to e^-large)
        w=np.exp(logits - np.max(logits)) # max 0
        wsum=w.sum()
        if wsum<=0: w=np.zeros_like(C); w[M.argmax()]=1.0
        else: w/=wsum
        G=np.zeros(n)
        # Only loop where w > 1e-5
        idx=np.nonzero(w > 1e-5)[0]
        for k in idx:
            wk=2.0*w[k]
            i0=max(0, k-(n-1)); i1=min(n-1, k)
            if i1<i0: continue
            j0=k-i1; j1=k-i0
            G[i0:i1+1] += wk * p[j0:j1+1]
        G *= 2.0 / M  # derivative of logsumexp(C/M): 1/M
        grad = p * (G - np.dot(p,G))
        # if grad all small break
        t += 1
        m=0.9*m+0.1*grad
        v=0.99*v+0.01*(grad*grad)
        mhat=m/(1-0.9**t); vhat=v/(1-0.99**t)
        step=lr*mhat/(np.sqrt(vhat)+1e-8)
        y_new=y-step
        # clip y_new for stability: y_new = np.clip(y_new, y.min()-10, y.max()+10)? 
        temp=np.exp(y_new - np.max(y_new))
        pnew=temp/temp.sum()
        s2=fast_score(pnew)
        if np.isfinite(s2) and s2 < best_s:
             p=pnew; y=y_new; best_s=s2; update state
        else:
             # try half step
             step*=0.5; y_new=y-step; temp=...; score; if improve accept else break/decrease lr
        if t % 10 ==0: maybe print
        if t>50000: break
```
This might be too slow due Python loop over idx for each iteration. But n=1024, idx maybe ~200, each slice length ≤n, total operations ~200k, 1000 iterations ~200M, maybe seconds to tens. Could be okay for time-limited. But if too slow, no improvement. We can skip Adam if not needed. But include.

For cross-correlation where output i = ∑_{k} w_k p_{k-i}. Let define w_shifted array length M with w reversed? Standard cross-correlation c[i]=∑_j a[j] b[j+i]. We have ∑_k w_k p[k-i]. Let's write top-level helper details:

```
def normalize(a, eps=1e-12):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64)
    a=np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    a=np.clip(a,0.0,None)
    if a.size==0:
       return a
    s=float(a.sum())
    if s<=1e-300:
       a=np.ones_like(a); s=float(a.size)
    a=a/s
    if eps>0:
       a=(1.0-eps)*a + eps/float(a.size)
       s=a.sum()
       if s>0: a=a/s
    return a
```
If eps too large? 1e-10 okay.

`fast_score` should not modify input? Use copy.
```
def fast_score(a):
  arr=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64)
  if arr.size==0: return inf
  arr=arr.ravel()
  arr=np.nan_to_num(arr,...); arr=np.clip(arr,0,None)
  s=arr.sum()
  if s<=1e-300: return inf
  p=arr/s
  n=p.size
  m=1
  target=2*n-1
  while m<target: m <<=1
  F=np.fft.rfft(p, m)
  c=np.fft.irfft(F*F, m)
  c=c[:target]
  # Remove tiny imaginary? irfft real.
  M=float(np.max(c)) if c.size else 0.0
  if not np.isfinite(M) or M<0:
     # fallback direct for small?
     if n<=2000:
       c=np.convolve(p,p)
       M=float(np.max(c))
     else: M=0
  return float(2.0*n*M)
```
For large n FFT error can produce tiny negatives but max positive. Good.

def run_lp_sequence(start, end_time, state, max_iters=200):
  rng=np.random.default_rng((int(time.monotonic()*1000000)%100000))
  p=normalize(start,1e-10)
  cur_s=fast_score(p)
  if finite and cur_s < state['score']: update_fast
  M=cur_s/(2*p.size)
  local_best=p; local_best_s=cur_s
  it=0; stagn=0; next_exact=time.monotonic()+30
  while time.monotonic() < end_time and it < max_iters:
    it +=1
    res=lp_update(p, M, rng)
    if res is None: break
    cand, cand_s = res
    improved = cand_s < cur_s - 1e-7
    if improved:
       p=cand; cur_s=cand_s; M=cur_s/(2*p.size); stagn=0
    elif allow_explore and cand_s < cur_s * (1.0 + 5e-4) and stagn > 3 and rng.random() < 0.3:
       p=cand; cur_s=cand_s; M=...
    else:
       stagn +=1
       if stagn > 15:
          # random perturb and reset stagn
          p=perturb(p,rng)
          cur_s=fast_score(p); M=cur_s/(2*p.size)
          stagn=5
          if time.monotonic() > end_time-break? 
    if cur_s < local_best_s:
       local_best=p.copy(); local_best_s=cur_s; update fast state
    if time.monotonic() > next_exact:
       update_exact_state(state, p, end_time)
       next_exact=time.monotonic()+60
  # final update state fast and exact if time
  update_fast_state(state, local_best)
  if time.monotonic() < end_time -10:
     update_exact_state(state, local_best, end_time)
```

`perturb(p,rng)`:
```
def perturb(p, rng):
  c=p.copy()
  n=p.size
  if n==0: return c
  width=int(rng.integers(max(1,min(64,n//50)), max(2,min(256,n//8)))) maybe
  width = int(max(2, min(n//8, rng.integers(2, max(3,n//20)))))
  start=int(rng.integers(0,n-width+1))
  factor=np.exp(rng.normal(0.0,0.25))
  c[start:start+width] *= factor
  if rng.random()<0.25:
     # shift by random integer
     k=int(rng.integers(1,n))
     c=np.roll(c,k)
  return normalize(c,1e-10)
```
Rolling breaks endpoints but allows shifts; okay.

def record_candidate(state, arr, limit=30):
  a=np.asarray(arr,float).ravel()
  cands=state.get('cands', [])
  # if similar to existing? skip heavy

  else:
    cands[-1]=normalize(a,0.0).copy()
  state['cands']=cands

`smooth_max_descent` code top-level:
```
def smooth_max_descent(p, end_time, state):
    cur=normalize(p,1e-10); s=fast_score(cur); best=cur; best_s=s; update state
    n=cur.size; eta=0.1; no=0; it=0
    while time.monotonic()<end_time and it<20000:
        it+=1
        C=conv_fft(cur); M=float(np.max(C)) if C.size else 0
        if M<=0 or not finite: break
        topk=max(8, min(128, n//8))
        idx=np.argpartition(C, -topk)[-topk:]
        vals=C[idx]
        tmp=vals - vals.max()
        # avoid divide by zero M*alpha
        denom=max(1e-12, M*0.1)
        w=np.exp(tmp/denom); w/=w.sum()
        G=np.zeros(n)
        # loop
        for ii in range(topk):
           k=int(idx[ii]); wk=2.0*float(w[ii])
           i0=max(0,k-(n-1)); i1=min(n-1,k)
           j0=k-i1; j1=k-i0
           G[i0:i1+1] += wk * cur[j0:j1+1]
        gm=G.max()
        if gm<=0 or not finite: break
        G=(G/gm) - ((G/gm).mean())
        best_step=None; best_sc=fast_score(cur)
        for e in (eta,0.5*eta,2.0*eta,0.1*eta):
           cand=cur*np.exp(-e*G)
           cand=np.maximum(cand,0); sc=fast_score(cand)
           if finite and sc<best_sc: best_sc=sc; best_step=e; best_cand=normalize(cand,1e-12)
        if best_step is None:
           no+=1
           eta*=0.5
           if no>50: break
        else:
           cur=best_cand; eta=best_step; s=best_sc; no=0
           if s<best_s: best=cur.copy(); best_s=s; update state
        if it%100==0: print
    update state best/exact if time
```
This can get stuck due topk only; okay.

def random_local_search(p, end_time, state):
  cur=normalize(p,1e-10); s=fast_score(cur); best=cur; best_s=s; update
  rng=...
  step=0.1; no=0
  while time<end:
    cand=cur.copy(); width=int(rng.integers(2, min(64, max(3, cur.size//32))))
    start=rng.integers(0,cur.size-width+1)
    factor=np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, step))
    cand[start:start+width]*=factor
    # sometimes scale a second block
    sc=fast_score(cand)
    if sc<s: accept else no++; if no>200 break; if no>100: step*=0.7
  return best

In gradient loop, `j0` and `j1` are indices into cur for j=k-i. For i0 to i1, j decreases. We want array order corresponding i0..i1. If i increases, j decreases. ```python
"""
Search for a non-negative step-function sequence minimizing
2*n*max(convolve(a,a))/(sum(a)^2).

The program combines several ideas:
  1. A collection of analytically motivated initial profiles.
  2. A local "max-convolution" gradient step that pushes mass away from
     the currently dominant convolution coefficients.
  3. The linear-programming fixed-point iteration described in the prompt:
       given f, maximize sum(g) subject to g >= 0 and f*g <= M,
     then mix f with a normalized version of g.
  4. Upsampling to longer sequences and final exact evaluation.

Only numpy/scipy are used when necessary.  The optimizer is intentionally
self-contained and robust: if an LP or FFT/SCIPY solve fails, the search
falls back to the previous best sequence.
"""

import time
import numpy as np

try:
    import scipy.sparse as sp
    import scipy.optimize as opt
    HAVE_SCIPY = True
except Exception:
    HAVE_SCIPY = False


def normalize_sequence(a, eps=0.0):
    """Clamp to non-negative, finite values and normalize sum to 1."""
    arr = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if arr.size == 0:
        return arr
    arr = np.nan_to_num(arr, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, None)
    s = float(np.sum(arr))
    if s <= 1e-300:
        arr = np.ones_like(arr)
        s = float(arr.size)
    arr = arr / s
    if eps > 0:
        arr = (1.0 - eps) * arr + eps / float(arr.size)
        s2 = float(np.sum(arr))
        if s2 > 0:
            arr = arr / s2
    return arr


def convolution_fft(a):
    """Full convolution using FFT.  Input should already be normalized."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = a.size
    need = 2 * n - 1
    m = 1
    while m < need:
        m <<= 1
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, m)
    out = np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, m)[:need]
    out = np.real(out)
    out[np.abs(out) < 1e-15] = 0.0
    return out


def fast_score(a):
    """Fast scale-invariant score; same objective as evaluate_sequence,
    but using FFT and with no external caller overhead."""
    arr = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if arr.size == 0:
        return np.inf
    arr = np.nan_to_num(arr, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, None)
    s = float(np.sum(arr))
    if s <= 1e-300:
        return np.inf
    p = arr / s
    n = p.size
    c = convolution_fft(p)
    if c.size == 0:
        return np.inf
    m = float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(m) or m < 0.0:
        if n <= 2500:
            c2 = np.convolve(p, p)
            m = float(np.max(c2)) if c2.size else 0.0
        else:
            m = 0.0
    if not np.isfinite(m):
        return np.inf
    return float(2.0 * n * m)


def get_global_sequence():
    try:
        seq = globals().get("height_sequence_1")
    except Exception:
        return None
    if seq is None:
        return None
    try:
        arr = np.asarray(seq, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    except Exception:
        return None
    if arr.size == 0:
        return None
    arr = np.nan_to_num(arr, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, None)
    if float(np.sum(arr)) <= 0.0:
        return None
    return arr


def resample_sequence(a, n_new):
    """Linear resampling, preserving non-negativity and scale-invariant shape."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n_old = a.size
    if n_new <= 0:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
    if n_old == 0:
        return np.ones(n_new, dtype=np.float64)
    if n_old == n_new:
        return a.copy()
    if n_old == 1:
        return np.full(n_new, float(a[0]), dtype=np.float64)
    x_old = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n_old)
    x_new = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n_new)
    b = np.interp(x_new, x_old, a)
    b = np.nan_to_num(b, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    b = np.clip(b, 0.0, None)
    return b


def smooth1d(a, sigma):
    """Small Gaussian smoothing; keeps non-negativity and sum 1."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = a.size
    if n == 0 or sigma <= 0:
        return a.copy()
    rad = int(max(1, 4 * float(sigma)) + 1)
    if rad % 2 == 0:
        rad += 1
    x = np.arange(-rad // 2, rad // 2 + 1, dtype=np.float64)
    ker = np.exp(-0.5 * (x / float(sigma)) ** 2)
    ks = float(ker.sum())
    if ks <= 0:
        return a.copy()
    ker /= ks
    b = np.convolve(a, ker, mode="same")
    b = np.clip(b, 0.0, None)
    bs = float(np.sum(b))
    if bs <= 0:
        return a.copy()
    return b / bs


def make_initial_candidates(n, rng):
    """Generate a variety of non-negative normalized profiles."""
    cands = []
    if n <= 0:
        return cands

    x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)

    def add_seq(v):
        vv = np.asarray(v, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
        vv = np.nan_to_num(vv, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
        vv = np.clip(vv, 0.0, None)
        s = float(np.sum(vv))
        if s > 1e-300:
            cands.append(vv / s)

    # Uniform baseline.
    add_seq(np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64))

    # Geometric ramps, both orientations.
    for alpha in (0.25, 0.5, 0.75, 1.0, 1.3, 1.7, 2.2, 3.0, 4.0, 6.0):
        g = np.exp(-alpha * x)
        add_seq(g)
        add_seq(g[::-1])

    # Asymmetric two-end exponentials.
    for lam, alpha, beta in (
        (0.05, 1.0, 1.0),
        (0.10, 1.0, 1.0),
        (0.18, 1.0, 1.0),
        (0.30, 1.3, 1.0),
        (0.45, 1.7, 0.9),
        (0.60, 2.0, 0.8),
        (0.80, 1.2, 1.0),
        (1.00, 1.0, 1.0),
    ):
        add_seq(np.exp(-alpha * x) + lam * np.exp(-beta * (1.0 - x)))

    # Gaussian bumps at several locations.
    for mu, sigma, alpha in (
        (0.00, 1.00, 1.00),
        (0.10, 0.70, 1.00),
        (0.25, 1.00, 1.00),
        (0.50, 1.00, 1.00),
        (0.75, 1.00, 1.00),
        (0.90, 0.70, 1.00),
        (0.35, 0.50, 1.60),
        (1.00, 0.90, 1.20),
    ):
        add_seq(np.exp(-alpha * ((x - mu) / sigma) ** 2))

    # Powers of the coordinate.
    for p in (0.3, 0.5, 1.0, 1.5, 2.0, 3.0, 4.0):
        add_seq(x ** p)
        add_seq((1.0 - x) ** p)

    # Low-amplitude cosine perturbations.
    for amp in (0.20, 0.45, 0.75, 0.95):
        for phi in (0.0, 0.35, 0.7, 1.05, 1.4, 1.75, 2.1, 2.5, 2.9, 3.3):
            v = 1.0 + amp * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * x + phi)
            v = np.maximum(v, 1e-6)
            add_seq(v)

    # Random smooth low-frequency profiles.
    for _ in range(8):
        v = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
        for k in range(1, 7):
            amp = float(rng.normal(0.0, 0.12))
            phase = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi))
            v += amp * (0.5 * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * k * x + phase)
                        + 0.5 * np.sin(2.0 * np.pi * k * x + phase))
        v = np.maximum(v, 1e-4)
        add_seq(v)

    # If the provided construction exists, include a resampled copy.
    g = get_global_sequence()
    if g is not None and g.size > 1:
        add_seq(resample_sequence(g, n))

    return cands


def record_candidate(state, arr, limit=30):
    """Keep a limited set of candidate arrays for final exact evaluation."""
    a = np.asarray(arr, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        return
    cands = state.get("cands", [])
    a = normalize_sequence(a, 0.0).copy()
    if len(cands) < limit:
        cands.append(a)
    else:
        cands[-1] = a
    state["cands"] = cands


def update_fast_state(state, arr):
    """Update fast-score best and maybe record a candidate."""
    a = np.asarray(arr, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        return
    sc = fast_score(a)
    if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < state["score"] - 1e-12:
        state["score"] = float(sc)
        b = normalize_sequence(a, 0.0).copy()
        state["seq"] = b
        record_candidate(state, b)
        print("fast best:", sc, "length", b.size)


def update_exact_state(state, arr, deadline=None):
    """Try exact evaluation; update exact best if possible."""
    a = np.asarray(arr, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0 or a.size > 8000:
        return
    ev = globals().get("evaluate_sequence")
    if ev is None:
        return
    try:
        out = [float(x) for x in a]
    except Exception:
        return
    try:
        sc = float(ev(out))
    except Exception:
        return
    if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < state.get("exact_score", np.inf) - 1e-12:
        state["exact_score"] = sc
        state["exact_seq"] = normalize_sequence(a, 0.0).copy()
        if deadline is None or time.monotonic() < deadline:
            print("exact best:", sc, "length", a.size)


def solve_lp_for_sequence(p, M):
    """Solve the LP:
       max sum(q), q >= 0, (p * q)_k <= M for all k.
    Returns q or None."""
    if not HAVE_SCIPY:
        return None
    p = normalize_sequence(p, 1e-12)
    n = p.size
    if n <= 0:
        return None
    L = 2 * n - 1
    if M <= 0:
        return None

    offsets = list(range(n))
    try:
        # Each diagonal offset d has value p[d].
        A = sp.diags([float(p[d]) for d in range(n)],
                     offsets=offsets,
                     shape=(L, n),
                     format="csc")
    except Exception:
        try:
            diags = []
            for d in range(n):
                length = n - d
                diags.append(np.full(length, float(p[d]), dtype=np.float64))
            A = sp.diags(diags, offsets=offsets, shape=(L, n), format="csc")
        except Exception:
            return None

    try:
        c_obj = -np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
        b_vec = float(M) * np.ones(L, dtype=np.float64)
        bounds = [(0.0, None)] * n
        res = opt.linprog(
            c_obj,
            A_ub=A,
            b_ub=b_vec,
            bounds=bounds,
            method="highs",
        )
        if res is not None and getattr(res, "success", False):
            q = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
            q = np.nan_to_num(q, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
            q = np.clip(q, 0.0, None)
            if float(np.sum(q)) > 0.0:
                return q
    except Exception:
        return None
    return None


def lp_mixed_update(p, M, rng):
    """One LP-derived update.  Returns (candidate, fast_score) or None."""
    q = solve_lp_for_sequence(p, M)
    if q is None:
        return None

    q = normalize_sequence(q, 1e-12)

    # Mild smoothing of the LP point; keeps it from being too spiky.
    n = p.size
    if n >= 256:
        sigma = max(1.0, n / 512.0)
        q_smooth = smooth1d(q, sigma)
        q = 0.25 * q_smooth + 0.75 * q
        q = normalize_sequence(q, 1e-12)

    best_cand = None
    best_score = np.inf

    # Linear mixes.
    for r in (0.05, 0.10, 0.20, 0.35, 0.55, 0.80, 1.00):
        cand = (1.0 - r) * p + r * q
        cand = np.maximum(cand, 0.0)
        sc = fast_score(cand)
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
            best_score = sc
            best_cand = normalize_sequence(cand, 1e-12)

    # Geometric-mean mixes (often stabler on logit/presence patterns).
    lp = np.maximum(p, 1e-12)
    lq = np.maximum(q, 1e-12)
    for r in (0.20, 0.45, 0.70):
        try:
            logcand = (1.0 - r) * np.log(lp) + r * np.log(lq)
            cand = np.exp(logcand - np.max(logcand))
        except Exception:
            continue
        cand = np.maximum(cand, 0.0)
        sc = fast_score(cand)
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
            best_score = sc
            best_cand = normalize_sequence(cand, 1e-12)

    if best_cand is None:
        sc = fast_score(q)
        if np.isfinite(sc):
            return normalize_sequence(q, 1e-12), float(sc)
        return None

    return best_cand, float(best_score)


def random_perturb(p, rng):
    """Small random block perturbation used to escape stagnation."""
    c = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).ravel().copy()
    n = c.size
    if n == 0:
        return c
    width = int(rng.integers(2, max(3, min(n, max(3, n // 25)))) )
    width = max(1, min(width, n))
    start = int(rng.integers(0, n - width + 1))
    factor = float(np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.22)))
    c[start:start + width] *= factor
    if n > 4 and rng.random() < 0.20:
        shift = int(rng.integers(1, n))
        c = np.roll(c, shift)
    return normalize_sequence(c, 1e-10)


def run_lp_sequence(start, end_time, state, max_iters=200):
    """Run LP-derived iterations from start until end_time.  Updates state."""
    rng = np.random.default_rng(int((time.monotonic() * 1000003) % 999999997))

    p = normalize_sequence(start, 1e-10)
    cur_score = fast_score(p)
    if not np.isfinite(cur_score):
        return p

    local_best = p.copy()
    local_best_score = cur_score
    update_fast_state(state, local_best)

    M = cur_score / (2.0 * p.size)
    if M <= 0:
        M = 1.0 / max(1, p.size)

    it = 0
    stagnation = 0
    next_exact = time.monotonic() + 30.0

    while time.monotonic() < end_time and it < max_iters:
        it += 1
        res = lp_mixed_update(p, M, rng)
        if res is None:
            break
        cand, cand_score = res
        cand = normalize_sequence(cand, 1e-10)
        if not np.isfinite(cand_score):
            break

        accepted = False
        if cand_score < cur_score - 1e-9:
            p = cand
            cur_score = cand_score
            M = cur_score / (2.0 * p.size)
            stagnation = 0
            accepted = True
        elif (cand_score < cur_score * 1.0005 and
              stagnation >= 4 and
              it < max(max_iters, 60) and
              rng.random() < 0.22):
            # Occasional gentle worsening to escape a flat region.
            p = cand
            cur_score = cand_score
            M = cur_score / (2.0 * p.size)
            stagnation += 1
            accepted = True

        if not accepted:
            stagnation += 1
            if stagnation > 18:
                p = random_perturb(p, rng)
                cur_score = fast_score(p)
                if not np.isfinite(cur_score):
                    break
                M = cur_score / (2.0 * p.size) if cur_score > 0 else 1.0 / p.size
                stagnation = 6

        if cur_score < local_best_score - 1e-12:
            local_best = p.copy()
            local_best_score = cur_score
            update_fast_state(state, local_best)

        if time.monotonic() > next_exact:
            update_exact_state(state, local_best, end_time)
            next_exact = time.monotonic() + 75.0

    if local_best_score < cur_score:
        update_fast_state(state, local_best)
    else:
        update_fast_state(state, p)
        local_best = p.copy()

    if time.monotonic() < max(0.0, end_time - 15.0):
        update_exact_state(state, local_best, end_time)

    print("LP stage done iters:", it, "local score:", local_best_score)
    return local_best


def smooth_max_descent(start, end_time, state):
    """Local gradient-like descent on a smoothed max-convolution objective."""
    rng = np.random.default_rng(int((time.monotonic() * 2654435761) % 999999997))
    cur = normalize_sequence(start, 1e-10)
    cur_score = fast_score(cur)
    if not np.isfinite(cur_score):
        return cur

    best = cur.copy()
    best_score = cur_score
    update_fast_state(state, best)

    n = cur.size
    eta = 0.10
    it = 0
    no_improve = 0
    next_exact = time.monotonic() + 45.0

    while time.monotonic() < end_time and it < 30000:
        it += 1
        c = convolution_fft(cur)
        if c.size == 0:
            break
        M = float(np.max(c))
        if not np.isfinite(M) or M <= 0:
            break

        topk = max(4, min(160, max(4, n // 8)))
        topk = min(topk, c.size)
        idx = np.argpartition(c, -topk)[-topk:]
        vals = c[idx]
        denom = max(1e-12, 0.08 * M)
        w = np.exp((vals - float(np.max(vals))) / denom)
        ws = float(np.sum(w))
        if ws <= 0:
            break
        w /= ws

        G = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
        for ii in range(topk):
            k = int(idx[ii])
            wk = 2.0 * float(w[ii])
            i0 = max(0, k - (n - 1))
            i1 = min(n - 1, k)
            if i1 < i0:
                continue
            j_hi = k - i0
            j_lo = k - i1
            if j_lo > 0:
                seg = cur[j_hi:j_lo - 1:-1]
            else:
                seg = cur[j_hi::-1]
            G[i0:i1 + 1] += wk * seg

        gmax = float(np.max(G))
        if gmax <= 0 or not np.isfinite(gmax):
            break
        G = G / gmax
        G = G - float(np.mean(G))

        cand_score = cur_score
        best_cand = None
        tries = (eta, 0.5 * eta, 2.0 * eta, 0.2 * eta, 0.05 * eta)
        for e in tries:
            if e <= 0:
                continue
            try:
                logp = np.log(np.maximum(cur, 1e-300))
                ncand = np.exp(logp - e * G)
            except Exception:
                continue
            ncand = np.maximum(ncand, 0.0)
            sc = fast_score(ncand)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < cand_score - 1e-13:
                cand_score = sc
                best_cand = normalize_sequence(ncand, 1e-12)

        if best_cand is None:
            no_improve += 1
            eta = max(1e-4, 0.6 * eta)
            if no_improve > 120:
                break
            continue

        cur = best_cand
        cur_score = cand_score
        eta = tries[0]
        no_improve = 0

        if cur_score < best_score - 1e-12:
            best = cur.copy()
            best_score = cur_score
            update_fast_state(state, best)

        if time.monotonic() > next_exact:
            update_exact_state(state, best, end_time)
            next_exact = time.monotonic() + 90.0

    update_fast_state(state, best)
    if time.monotonic() < end_time:
        update_exact_state(state, best, end_time)
    print("smooth descent done iters:", it, "best score:", best_score)
    return best


def random_local_search(start, end_time, state):
    """Small Monte-Carlo search around a good point."""
    rng = np.random.default_rng(int((time.monotonic() * 855441111) % 999999997))
    cur = normalize_sequence(start, 1e-10)
    cur_score = fast_score(cur)
    if not np.isfinite(cur_score):
        return cur

    best = cur.copy()
    best_score = cur_score
    update_fast_state(state, best)

    n = cur.size
    noise = 0.10
    no_improve = 0
    it = 0

    while time.monotonic() < end_time and it < 50000:
        it += 1
        cand = cur.copy()
        width = int(rng.integers(2, max(3, min(n, max(3, n // 40)))) )
        width = max(1, min(width, n))
        start_i = int(rng.integers(0, n - width + 1))
        factor = float(np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, noise)))
        cand[start_i:start_i + width] *= factor

        if rng.random() < 0.15:
            width2 = int(rng.integers(2, max(3, min(n, max(3, n // 25)))) )
            width2 = max(1, min(width2, n))
            start2 = int(rng.integers(0, n - width2 + 1))
            cand[start2:start2 + width2] *= float(np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, noise)))

        sc = fast_score(cand)
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < cur_score - 1e-12:
            cur = normalize_sequence(cand, 1e-10)
            cur_score = sc
            no_improve = 0
            if sc < best_score - 1e-12:
                best = cur.copy()
                best_score = sc
                update_fast_state(state, best)
        else:
            no_improve += 1
            if no_improve == 80:
                noise = max(0.02, noise * 0.7)
            if no_improve > 800:
                break

    update_fast_state(state, best)
    if time.monotonic() < end_time:
        update_exact_state(state, best, end_time)
    print("random local search done iters:", it, "best score:", best_score)
    return best


def finalize_candidates(state, deadline):
    """Evaluate a set of promising sequences with the authoritative evaluator."""
    arrs = []
    seen = set()

    def add(arr):
        a = np.asarray(arr, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
        if a.size == 0 or a.size > 8000:
            return
        try:
            key = (a.size, round(float(a.sum()), 12), float(np.max(a)),
                   float(np.min(a)), int(sum((a % 1) == 0)))
        except Exception:
            key = None
        if key is not None and key in seen:
            return
        if key is not None:
            seen.add(key)
        arrs.append(normalize_sequence(a, 0.0))

    add(state.get("seq"))
    add(state.get("exact_seq"))
    g = get_global_sequence()
    if g is not None:
        add(g)
        add(resample_sequence(g, 1024))
        add(resample_sequence(g, 2048))
        add(resample_sequence(g, 4096))

    for a in state.get("cands", []):
        add(a)

    base = state.get("seq")
    if base is not None:
        for m_new in (1024, 2048, 4096):
            add(resample_sequence(base, m_new))

    scored = []
    for i, a in enumerate(arrs):
        sc = fast_score(a)
        if np.isfinite(sc):
            scored.append((sc, i, a))
    scored.sort()

    limit = 25
    cnt = 0
    for sc, i, a in scored:
        if time.monotonic() > deadline - 5.0:
            break
        if cnt >= limit:
            break
        if a.size <= 6000:
            update_exact_state(state, a, deadline)
            cnt += 1


def to_return_list(seq, scale=10.0):
    """Convert an internal normalized sequence into a safe list."""
    a = np.asarray(seq, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        return [1.0] * 256
    a = np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, None)
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if s <= 1e-300:
        return [1.0] * a.size
    a = a * (scale / s)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    if float(np.sum(a)) <= 0.01:
        return [1.0] * a.size
    return [float(x) for x in a]


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    """Return the best sequence found within the time budget."""
    t_start = time.monotonic()
    total_budget = max(30.0, float(budget_s) if budget_s is not None else 2400.0)
    deadline = t_start + total_budget - 75.0

    state = {
        "score": np.inf,
        "seq": None,
        "exact_score": np.inf,
        "exact_seq": None,
        "cands": [],
    }

    # If the provided sequence is already excellent, keep it in mind.
    g = get_global_sequence()
    if g is not None:
        update_exact_state(state, g, deadline)

    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)

    # Main work length.  1024 is a good trade-off for LP and FFT.
    n_work = 1024
    inits = make_initial_candidates(n_work, rng)

    init_evals = []
    for idx, a in enumerate(inits):
        a = normalize_sequence(a, 1e-12)
        sc = fast_score(a)
        if np.isfinite(sc):
            init_evals.append((sc, idx, a))
            update_fast_state(state, a)
    init_evals.sort()

    top = []
    for score, idx, a in init_evals:
        if len(top) >= 8:
            break
        top.append(a)

    if not top:
        top = [normalize_sequence(np.ones(n_work), 1e-12)]

    # If we have very little time, just try the best initial sequence.
    if deadline - t_start < 240.0:
        best_init = top[0]
        update_exact_state(state, best_init, deadline)
        return to_return_list(state["seq"] if state["seq"] is not None else best_init)

    # Stage 1: quick smooth descent on promising initializations.
    stage1_deadline = min(deadline - 1700.0, t_start + 240.0)
    if stage1_deadline > time.monotonic() + 10.0:
        per1 = max(10.0, (stage1_deadline - t_start) / float(len(top)))
        smoothed = []
        for a in top:
            if time.monotonic() >= stage1_deadline:
                smoothed.append(a)
                continue
            end = min(stage1_deadline, time.monotonic() + per1)
            b = smooth_max_descent(a, end, state)
            if b is not None and b.size == a.size:
                smoothed.append(b)
            else:
                smoothed.append(a)
        top = smoothed

    # Re-rank after smoothing.
    rerank = []
    for idx, a in enumerate(top):
        sc = fast_score(a)
        if np.isfinite(sc):
            rerank.append((sc, idx, a))
    rerank.sort()
    top = [a for sc, idx, a in rerank]

    # Stage 2: LP-derived fixed-point search at n_work.
    stage2_deadline = min(deadline - 1050.0, t_start + 1700.0)
    if stage2_deadline > time.monotonic() + 30.0:
        per2 = max(25.0, (stage2_deadline - t_start) / float(len(top)))
        improved = []
        for a in top:
            if time.monotonic() >= stage2_deadline:
                improved.append(a)
                continue
            end = min(stage2_deadline, time.monotonic() + per2)
            b = run_lp_sequence(a, end, state, max_iters=160)
            if b is not None and b.size == n_work:
                improved.append(b)
            else:
                improved.append(a)
        top = improved

    # Choose the currently best array from state (fast) or top.
    if state.get("seq") is not None:
        cur_best = state["seq"]
    else:
        cur_best = top[0] if top else np.ones(n_work)

    # Stage 3: upscale and run a shorter LP search, if time allows.
    if time.monotonic() < deadline - 500.0:
        big_n = 2048
        cur_best = resample_sequence(cur_best, big_n)
        end = min(deadline - 350.0, time.monotonic() + 420.0)
        cur_best = run_lp_sequence(cur_best, end, state, max_iters=45)
        if cur_best is None or cur_best.size != big_n:
            cur_best = resample_sequence(state["seq"] if state["seq"] is not None else np.ones(n_work), big_n)

    # Stage 4: final purification at the currently best length.
    if time.monotonic() < deadline - 220.0:
        end = min(deadline - 160.0, time.monotonic() + 180.0)
        cur_best = smooth_max_descent(cur_best, end, state)

    # Stage 5: light local search.
    if time.monotonic() < deadline - 90.0:
        end = min(deadline - 70.0, time.monotonic() + 70.0)
        cur_best = random_local_search(cur_best, end, state)

    # Record the final candidate and exact-evaluate a shortlist.
    if cur_best is not None:
        record_candidate(state, cur_best, 40)
        update_exact_state(state, cur_best, deadline)

    finalize_candidates(state, deadline)

    if state.get("exact_seq") is not None:
        return to_return_list(state["exact_seq"])
    if state.get("seq") is not None:
        return to_return_list(state["seq"])
    if cur_best is not None:
        return to_return_list(cur_best)
    return [1.0] * 256
```
